p p -Poincaré inequalities and cutoff Sobolev inequalities on metric measure spaces
Meng Yang

Abstract
For p > 1 p>1 , we introduce the cutoff Sobolev inequality on general metric measure spaces, and prove that there exists a metric measure space endowed with a p p -energy that satisfies the chain condition, the volume regular condition with respect to a doubling scaling function Φ \Phi , and that both the Poincaré inequality and the the cutoff Sobolev inequality with respect to a doubling scaling function Ψ \Psi hold if and only if 1 C ( R r )^p≤ Ψ(R) Ψ(r) ≤C( R r )^p-1 Φ(R) Φ(r) for any r≤R. In particular, given any pair of doubling functions Φ \Phi and Ψ \Psi satisfying the above inequality, we construct a metric measure space endowed with a p p -energy on which all the above conditions are satisfied. As a direct corollary, we prove that there exists a metric measure space which is d h d_{h} -Ahlfors regular and has p p -walk dimension β p \beta_{p} if and only if p≤ β _p≤d_h+(p-1). Our proof builds on the Laakso-type space theory, which was recently developed by Murugan [ Ann. Probab. , to appear].

1
Introduction

On a complete non-compact Riemannian manifold, it was independently discovered by Grigor’yan
[
26
]
and Saloff-Coste
[
46
,
47
]
that the following two-sided Gaussian estimate of the heat kernel

\frac{C_{1}}{V(x,\sqrt{t})}\exp\left(-C_{2}\frac{d(x,y)^{2}}{t}\right)\leq p_{t}(x,y)\leq\frac{C_{3}}{V(x,\sqrt{t})}\exp\left(-C_{4}\frac{d(x,y)^{2}}{t}\right),

where
d(x,y)
is the geodesic distance,
V(x,r)=m(B(x,r))
is the Riemannian measure of the open ball
B(x,r)
, is equivalent to the conjunction of the volume doubling condition and the following Poincaré inequality

\int_{B(x,r)}|f-f_{B(x,r)}|^{2}\mathrm{d}m\leq Cr^{2}\int_{B(x,2r)}|\nabla f|^{2}\mathrm{d}m,

where
f_{B(x,r)}
is the mean value of
f
over
B(x,r)
. However, on many fractals, including the Sierpiński gasket and the Sierpiński carpet, the heat kernel has the following two-sided sub-Gaussian estimate

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}}{V(x,t^{1/\beta})}\exp\left(-C_{2}\left(\frac{d(x,y)}{t^{1/\beta}}\right)^{\frac{\beta}{\beta-1}}\right)\leq p_{t}(x,y)\leq\frac{C_{3}}{V(x,t^{1/\beta})}\exp\left(-C_{4}\left(\frac{d(x,y)}{t^{1/\beta}}\right)^{\frac{\beta}{\beta-1}}\right),

where
\beta
is a new parameter called the walk dimension, which is always strictly greater than 2 on fractals. For example,
\beta=\log 5/\log 2
on the Sierpiński gasket (see
[
13
,
36
]
),
\beta\approx 2.09697
on the Sierpiński carpet (see
[
6
,
7
,
12
,
8
,
38
,
32
]
). On general metric measure spaces that may exhibit different scaling behavior, such as fractal-like domains and fractal-like cable systems (see
[
9
,
11
]
), the heat kernel would display scale-dependent behavior as follows.

\frac{C_{1}}{V\left(x,\Psi^{-1}(t)\right)}\exp\left(-\Upsilon\left(C_{2}d(x,y),t\right)\right)\leq p_{t}(x,y)\leq\frac{C_{3}}{V\left(x,\Psi^{-1}(t)\right)}\exp\left(-\Upsilon\left(C_{4}d(x,y),t\right)\right),

HK(
\Psi
)

where

\Upsilon(R,t)=\sup_{s\in(0,+\infty)}\left(\frac{R}{s}-\frac{t}{\Psi(s)}\right),

here
\Psi
is a scaling function, which is also assumed to be doubling.
1
corresponds to the homogeneous case
\Psi(r)=r^{\beta}
. It was proved in
[
10
,
11
,
30
]
that on a general metric measure space, under the volume doubling condition,
HK(

Ψ

)
is equivalent to the conjunction of the following Poincaré inequality

\int_{B(x,r)}|f-f_{B(x,r)}|^{2}\mathrm{d}m\leq C\Psi(r)\int_{B(x,2r)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma(f,f),

where
\Gamma(f,f)
is the energy measure of
f
(see
[
25
]
for more details), and the cutoff Sobolev inequality or the generalized capacity condition, with the scaling function
\Psi
. It was proved in
[
42
]
that the cutoff Sobolev inequality can be replaced by the following simplified version. There exist
C_{1},C_{2}>0
,
A>1
, such that for any ball
B(x,r)
, there exists a cutoff function
\phi
for
B(x,r)\subseteq B(x,Ar)
such that for any
f
, we have

\int_{B(x,Ar)}|\widetilde{f}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\Gamma(\phi,\phi)\leq C_{1}\int_{B(x,Ar)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma(f,f)+\frac{C_{2}}{\Psi(r)}\int_{B(x,Ar)}|f|^{2}\mathrm{d}m,

where
\widetilde{f}
is a quasi-continuous modification of
f
.

However,
HK(

Ψ

)
would impose certain constraints on
\Psi
. In the homogeneous case, assume that the space is
d_{h}
-Ahlfors regular for some
d_{h}>0
, it was proved in
[
28
, Corollary 4.9]
that under the chain condition
1
1

1

It was recently proved in
[
41
, Corollary 1.8]
that the chain condition can be dropped.
,
1
implies

2\leq\beta\leq d_{h}+1,

see also
[
5
, Theorem 1]
for the infinite graph case. In the general case, assume that the space satisfies the volume regular condition V(
\Phi
)
with a scaling function
\Phi
, that is,
V(x,r)\asymp\Phi(r)
. Here
\Phi
is also assumed to be doubling. It was recently proved in
[
43
]
that
HK(

Ψ

)
implies

\frac{1}{C}\left(\frac{R}{r}\right)^{2}\leq\frac{\Psi(R)}{\Psi(r)}\leq C\frac{R\Phi(R)}{r\Phi(r)}\text{ for any }r\leq R.

A natural question is whether the converse also holds. In the homogeneous case, for any
d_{h},\beta
satisfying
2\leq\beta\leq d_{h}+1
, Barlow
[
5
]
constructed a
d_{h}
-Ahlfors regular space on which
1
holds. He used a construction similar to the Laakso space in
[
39
]
, adapted to the graph setting. In the general case, for any pair of doubling functions
\Phi,\Psi
satisfying the above inequality, Murugan
[
43
]
recently constructed a metric measure space on which both
V(
\Phi
)
and
HK(

Ψ

)
hold. He developed a general Laakso-type space theory, and proved that the Poincaré inequality and the cutoff Sobolev inequality, whose conjunction is equivalent to
HK(

Ψ

)
, holds on this space.

The preceding results are formulated within the Dirichlet form framework, which generalizes the classical Dirichlet integral
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\nabla f(x)|^{2}\mathrm{d}x
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. For general
p>1
, extending the classical
p
-energy
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\nabla f(x)|^{p}\mathrm{d}x
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, as initiated by
[
33
]
, the study of
p
-energy on fractals and general metric measure spaces has been recently advanced considerably, see
[
18
,
50
,
15
,
45
,
37
,
20
,
1
,
3
]
. In this setting, a new parameter
\beta_{p}
, called the
p
-walk dimension, naturally arises in connection with a
p
-energy. Notably,
\beta_{2}
coincides with
\beta
in
1
. However, for general
p>1
, there is no known analogue of the heat kernel estimate, instead, the
p
-walk dimension can be characterized in terms of the following Poincaré inequality

\displaystyle\int_{B(x,r)}|f-f_{B(x,r)}|^{p}\mathrm{d}m\leq Cr^{\beta_{p}}\int_{B(x,2r)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma(f),

PI(
\beta_{p}
)

where
\Gamma(f)
is the
p
-energy measure of
f
, and the following cutoff Sobolev inequality

\displaystyle\int_{B(x,Ar)}|\widetilde{f}|^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma(\phi)\leq C_{1}\int_{B(x,Ar)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma(f)+\frac{C_{2}}{r^{\beta_{p}}}\int_{B(x,Ar)}|f|^{p}\mathrm{d}m,

CS(
\beta_{p}
)

where
\phi
is a cutoff function for
B(x,r)\subseteq B(x,Ar)
. By taking
f\equiv 1
in
B(x,Ar)
, it is easy to see that
CS(

β
p

)
implies the following capacity upper bound

\displaystyle\mathrm{cap}(B(x,r),X\backslash B(x,Ar))\leq C_{2}\frac{V(x,Ar)}{r^{\beta_{p}}},

cap
(\beta_{p})_{\leq}

where
\mathrm{cap}(\cdot,\cdot)
is the capacity associated with a
p
-energy. On general metric measure spaces, scale-dependent formulations of the Poincaré inequaity PI
(\Psi)
, the cutoff Sobolev inequality CS
(\Psi)
, and the capacity upper bound cap
(\Psi)_{\leq}
can be considered. Similar to the case
p=2
, one can ask the following question.

Question 1:
In the homogeneous case, assume that the space is
d_{h}
-Ahlfors regular for some
d_{h}>0
, and that both
PI(

β
p

)
and
CS(

β
p

)
hold. What are the constraints on
d_{h}
and
\beta_{p}
? In the general case, assume that
V(
\Phi
)
,
PI
(\Psi)
and
CS
(\Psi)
hold. What are the constraints on
\Phi
and
\Psi
?

In the homogeneous case, it was proved in
[
14
,
51
]
that under the chain condition, the conjunction of
PI(

β
p

)
and
cap

(

β
p

)

≤

implies

p\leq\beta_{p}\leq d_{h}+(p-1).

The initial purpose of this paper is to consider the general case. We will prove that under the chain condition, the conjunction of
V(
\Phi
)
,
PI
(\Psi)
and
cap
(\Psi)_{\leq}
implies

\frac{1}{C}\left(\frac{R}{r}\right)^{p}\leq\frac{\Psi(R)}{\Psi(r)}\leq C\left(\frac{R}{r}\right)^{p-1}\frac{\Phi(R)}{\Phi(r)}\text{ for any }r\leq R,

which is Proposition
2.1
. It is also natural to ask whether the converse holds as follows.

Question 2:
For any pair of doubling functions
\Phi,\Psi
satisfying the above inequality, does there exist a metric measure space endowed with a
p
-energy, on which
V(
\Phi
)
,
PI
(\Psi)
and
CS
(\Psi)
hold?

The main purpose of this paper is to give a positive answer to this question, which is Theorem
2.3
. Very recently, Eriksson-Bique
[
22
]
proved that, under the volume doubling condition and
PI
(\Psi)
,
CS
(\Psi)
and
cap
(\Psi)_{\leq}
are equivalent. This result resolves the long-standing resistance conjecture (see
[
29
]
). See also Murugan
[
44
]
for the resolution of the resistance conjecture in the non-local setting (see
[
27
]
). However, in the present paper, we will establish
CS
(\Psi)

directly
, without relying on these results.

As an application of our results, we will consider the critical exponent
\alpha_{p}
of Besov spaces
B^{p,\alpha}
for
p>1
, which was recently studied in
[
2
,
14
]
. We will provide a characterization of the range in which the critical exponent
\alpha_{p}
is attainable.

To conclude the introduction, let us give a brief overview of our solution to Question 2 based on the Laakso-type space theory developed in
[
43
]
.

Firstly, we use an
\mathbb{R}
-tree
\mathcal{T}(\mathbf{b})
introduced in
[
43
]
, where
\mathbf{b}
is a function that determines the branching numbers of the tree and is thus referred to as a branching function. Owing to the tree property, suitable differential structure can be introduced on
\mathcal{T}(\mathbf{b})
, from which we can construct a
p
-energy, obtain the Poincaré inequality and the capacity upper bound in a relatively straightforward manner. See also
[
15
,
16
,
20
]
for the Vicsek set case. However, in contrast to the case
p=2
, which was considered in
[
43
]
and for which numerous equivalent characterizations of heat kernel estimates are available, we will prove the cutoff Sobolev inequality
directly
.

Secondly, an ultrametric space
\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{g})
is introduced to define a product space
\mathcal{P}(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{b})=\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{g})\times\mathcal{T}(\mathbf{b})
. The Laakso-type space
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{b})
is then obtained as the quotient of
\mathcal{P}(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{b})
with respect to an suitable equivalence relation, or roughly speaking, by gluing different copies of
\mathcal{T}(\mathbf{b})
along “carefully” chosen “wormholes” determined by the function
\mathbf{g}
. For this reason,
\mathbf{g}
is referred to as a gluing function. We will introduce a metric
\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{b})}
, which is slightly different from the one used in
[
43
]
. We will show that
(\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{b}),\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{b})})
is a geodesic space and give a characterization of its geodesics, a topic not addressed in
[
43
]
. Let
\mathbf{m}_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{b})}
be the pushforward of the product measure on
\mathcal{P}(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{b})
, then
(\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{b}),\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{b})},\mathbf{m}_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{b})})
is a metric measure space on which
V(
\Phi
)
holds, where
\Phi=V_{\mathbf{g}}V_{\mathbf{b}}
and
V_{\mathbf{g}},V_{\mathbf{b}}
are determined by
\mathbf{g},\mathbf{b}
, respectively.

Thirdly, due to the ultrametric property of
\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{g})
and the “carefully” chosen “wormholes” in
\mathcal{T}(\mathbf{b})
, the differential structure on
\mathcal{T}(\mathbf{b})
can be transferred to
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{b})
, from which we construct a
p
-energy on
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{b})
. We will prove the Poincaré inequality
PI
(\Psi)
using the technique of the pencil of curves, following an argument similar to that in
[
43
]
, adapted to the
p
-energy setting, and the cutoff Sobolev inequality
CS
(\Psi)
, the capacity upper bound
cap
(\Psi)_{\leq}
using the corresponding result on
\mathcal{T}(\mathbf{b})
, here
\Psi
is given by
\Psi(r)=r^{p-1}V_{\mathbf{b}}(r)
. To complete the proof, it remains to find
\mathbf{g},\mathbf{b}
corresponding to given
\Phi,\Psi
.

Throughout this paper,
p>1
is fixed. The letters
C,C_{1},C_{2},C_{A},C_{B}
will always refer to some positive constants and may change at each occurrence. The sign
\asymp
means that the ratio of the two sides is bounded from above and below by positive constants. The sign
\lesssim
(
\gtrsim
) means that the LHS is bounded by positive constant times the RHS from above (below). We will use the notation
\lfloor x\rfloor
(
\lceil x\rceil
) to denote the largest integer less than or equal to (the smallest integer greater than or equal to)
x\in\mathbb{R}
. We use
\#A
to denote the cardinality of a set
A
.

2
Statement of main results

We say that a function
\Phi:[0,+\infty)\to[0,+\infty)
is doubling if
\Phi
is a homeomorphism, which implies that
\Phi
is strictly increasing continuous and
\Phi(0)=0
, and there exists
C_{\Phi}>1
, called a doubling constant of
\Phi
, such that
\Phi(2r)\leq C_{\Phi}\Phi(r)
for any
r>0
. It follows directly that for any
R,r>0
with
r\leq R
, we have
\frac{\Phi(R)}{\Phi(r)}\leq C_{\Phi}\left(\frac{R}{r}\right)^{\log_{2}C_{\Phi}}
. Throughout this paper, we always assume that
\Phi,\Psi
are two doubling functions with doubling constants
C_{\Phi},C_{\Psi}
, respectively. Moreover, we assume that there exist
\beta^{(1)}_{\Psi},\beta^{(2)}_{\Psi}>0
with
\beta^{(1)}_{\Psi}\leq\beta^{(2)}_{\Psi}
such that

\frac{1}{C_{\Psi}}\left(\frac{R}{r}\right)^{\beta^{(1)}_{\Psi}}\leq\frac{\Psi(R)}{\Psi(r)}\leq C_{\Psi}\left(\frac{R}{r}\right)^{\beta^{(2)}_{\Psi}}\text{ for any }r\leq R.

(2.1)

Indeed, we can take
\beta^{(2)}_{\Psi}=\log_{2}C_{\Psi}
.

Let
(X,d,m)
be a metric measure space, that is,
(X,d)
is a locally compact separable metric space and
m
is a positive Radon measure on
X
with full support. For any
x\in X
and any
r>0
, denote
B(x,r)=\{y\in X:d(x,y)<r\}
,
\overline{B}(x,r)=\{y\in X:d(x,y)\leq r\}
, and
V(x,r)=m(B(x,r))
. If
B=B(x,r)
, then denote
\delta B=B(x,\delta r)
for any
\delta>0
. Let
\mathrm{diam}(X)=\sup\{d(x,y):x,y\in X\}
be the diameter of
(X,d)
. Let
\mathcal{B}(X)
be the family of all Borel measurable subsets of
X
. Let
C(X)
be the family of all continuous functions on
X
. Let
C_{c}(X)
be the family of all continuous functions on
X
with compact support. Denote
\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{A}=\frac{1}{m(A)}\int_{A}
and
u_{A}=\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{A}u\mathrm{d}m
for any measurable set
A
with
m(A)\in(0,+\infty)
and any function
u
such that the integral
\int_{A}u\mathrm{d}m
is well-defined.

We say that the chain condition
CC
holds if there exists
C_{cc}>0
such that for any
x,y\in X
and any positive integer
n
, there exists a sequence
\{x_{k}:0\leq k\leq n\}
of points in
X
with
x_{0}=x
and
x_{n}=y
such that

d(x_{k},x_{k-1})\leq C_{cc}\frac{d(x,y)}{n}\text{ for any }k=1,\ldots,n.

CC

A simple but useful consequence of
CC
is that for any open interval
(a,b)\subseteq(0,\mathrm{diam}(X))
, there exist
x,y\in X
such that
d(x,y)\in(a,b)
.

We say that the volume doubling condition
VD
holds if there exists
C_{VD}>0
such that

V(x,2r)\leq C_{VD}V(x,r)\text{ for any }x\in X,r>0.

VD

We say that the volume regular condition
V(

Φ

)
holds if there exists
C_{VR}>0
such that

\frac{1}{C_{VR}}\Phi(r)\leq V(x,r)\leq C_{VR}\Phi(r)\text{ for any }x\in X,r\in(0,\mathrm{diam}(X)).

V(
\Phi
)

For
d_{h}>0
, we say that the Ahlfors regular condition V(
d_{h}
)
holds if
V(

Φ

)
holds with
\Phi:r\mapsto r^{d_{h}}
.

We say that
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
is a
p
-energy on
(X,d,m)
if
\mathcal{F}
is a dense subspace of
L^{p}(X;m)
and
\mathcal{E}:\mathcal{F}\to[0,+\infty)
satisfies the following conditions.

(1)

\mathcal{E}^{1/p}
is a semi-norm on
\mathcal{F}
, that is, for any
f,g\in\mathcal{F}
and any
c\in\mathbb{R}
, we have
\mathcal{E}(f)\geq 0
,
\mathcal{E}(cf)^{1/p}=|c|\mathcal{E}(f)^{1/p}
, and
\mathcal{E}(f+g)^{1/p}\leq\mathcal{E}(f)^{1/p}+\mathcal{E}(g)^{1/p}
.

(2)

(Closed property)
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{E}(\cdot)^{1/p}+\lVert\cdot\rVert_{L^{p}(X;m)})
is a Banach space.

(3)

(Markovian property) For any
\varphi\in C(\mathbb{R})
with
\varphi(0)=0
and
|\varphi(t)-\varphi(s)|\leq|t-s|
for any
t,s\in\mathbb{R}
, for any
f\in\mathcal{F}
, we have
\varphi(f)\in\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{E}(\varphi(f))\leq\mathcal{E}(f)
.

(4)

(Regular property)
\mathcal{F}\cap C_{c}(X)
is uniformly dense in
C_{c}(X)
and
(\mathcal{E}(\cdot)^{1/p}+\lVert\cdot\rVert_{L^{p}(X;m)})
-dense in
\mathcal{F}
.

(5)

(Strongly local property) For any
f,g\in\mathcal{F}
with compact support and
g
constant in an open neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}(f)
, we have
\mathcal{E}(f+g)=\mathcal{E}(f)+\mathcal{E}(g)
.

(6)

(
p
-Clarkson’s inequality) For any
f,g\in\mathcal{F}
, we have

\begin{cases}\mathcal{E}(f+g)+\mathcal{E}(f-g)\geq 2\left(\mathcal{E}(f)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}+\mathcal{E}(g)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right)^{p-1}&\text{if }p\in(1,2],\\
\mathcal{E}(f+g)+\mathcal{E}(f-g)\leq 2\left(\mathcal{E}(f)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}+\mathcal{E}(g)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right)^{p-1}&\text{if }p\in[2,+\infty).\\
\end{cases}

Cla

For
p=2
, the above definition coincides with the definition of strongly local regular Dirichlet forms.

By
[
48
, Theorem 2.4]
, a
p
-energy
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
corresponds to a (canonical)
p
-energy measure
\Gamma:\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{B}(X)\to[0,+\infty)
,
(f,A)\mapsto\Gamma(f)(A)
satisfying the following conditions.

(1)

For any
f\in\mathcal{F}
,
\Gamma(f)(\cdot)
is a positive Radon measure on
X
with
\Gamma(f)(X)=\mathcal{E}(f)
.

(2)

For any
A\in\mathcal{B}(X)
,
\Gamma(\cdot)(A)^{1/p}
is a semi-norm on
\mathcal{F}
.

(3)

For any
f,g\in\mathcal{F}\cap C_{c}(X)
,
A\in\mathcal{B}(X)
, if
f-g
is constant on
A
, then
\Gamma(f)(A)=\Gamma(g)(A)
.

(4)

(
p
-Clarkson’s inequality) For any
f,g\in\mathcal{F}
and any
A\in\mathcal{B}(X)
, we have

\begin{cases}\Gamma(f+g)(A)+\Gamma(f-g)(A)\geq 2\left(\Gamma(f)(A)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}+\Gamma(g)(A)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right)^{p-1}&\text{if }p\in(1,2],\\
\Gamma(f+g)(A)+\Gamma(f-g)(A)\leq 2\left(\Gamma(f)(A)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}+\Gamma(g)(A)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right)^{p-1}&\text{if }p\in[2,+\infty).\\
\end{cases}

(5)

(Chain rule) For any
f\in\mathcal{F}\cap C_{c}(X)
, for any piecewise
C^{1}
function
\varphi:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
, we have
\mathrm{d}\Gamma(\varphi(f))=|\varphi^{\prime}(f)|^{p}\mathrm{d}\Gamma(f)
.

For
p=2
, the above definition coincides with the definition of energy measures with respect to strongly local regular Dirichlet forms. Using the chain rule, we have the following conditions.

(1)

(Strong sub-additivity) For any
f,g\in\mathcal{F}
, we have
f\vee g,f\wedge g\in\mathcal{F}
and

\mathcal{E}(f\vee g)+\mathcal{E}(f\wedge g)\leq\mathcal{E}(f)+\mathcal{E}(g).

SubAdd

(2)

(
\mathcal{F}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
is an algebra) For any
f,g\in\mathcal{F}\cap L^{\infty}(X;m)
, we have
fg\in\mathcal{F}
and

\mathcal{E}(fg)^{{1}/{p}}\leq C_{p}\max\left\{\lVert f\rVert_{L^{\infty}(X;m)},\lVert g\rVert_{L^{\infty}(X;m)}\right\}\left(\mathcal{E}(g)^{1/p}+\mathcal{E}(f)^{1/p}\right),

Alg

.

Our first result is that certain geometric and functional conditions impose constraints on doubling functions as follows.

Proposition 2.1
.

a

In particular, assume that

Remark 2.2

Our main result is that the “converse” of Proposition

also holds as follows.

Theorem 2.3
.

Assume that there exists
C>0
such that for any
R,r>0
with

, we have

\frac{1}{C}\left(\frac{R}{r}\right)^{p}\leq\frac{\Psi(R)}{\Psi(r)}\leq C\left(\frac{R}{r}\right)^{p-1}\frac{\Phi(R)}{\Phi(r)}.

Then there exists an unbounded geodesic metric measure space
(X,d,m)
and a
p
-energy
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
with a
p
-energy measure
\Gamma
such that
V(

Φ

)
,

Ψ

)
and

Ψ

)
hold. In particular, for any
d_{h},\beta_{p}>0
satisfying

p\leq\beta_{p}\leq d_{h}+(p-1),

there exists an unbounded geodesic metric measure space
(X,d,m)
and a
p
-energy
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
with a
p
-energy measure
\Gamma
such that
V(
d_{h}
)
,
PI(
\beta_{p}
)
and
\text{CS}(\beta_{p})
hold.

As an application, we consider the critical exponent of Besov spaces as follows. For any
\alpha>0
, we have the following definition of Besov spaces.

\displaystyle B^{p,\alpha}(X,d,m)

\displaystyle=\left\{f\in L^{p}(X;m):\sup_{r\in(0,\mathrm{diam}(X))}\frac{1}{r^{p\alpha}}\int_{X}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x,r)}|f(x)-f(y)|^{p}m(\mathrm{d}y)m(\mathrm{d}x)<+\infty\right\}.

Obviously,
B^{p,\alpha}(X,d,m)
is decreasing in
\alpha
and may become trivial if
\alpha
is too large. We define the following critical exponent

\alpha_{p}(X,d,m)=\sup\left\{\alpha>0:B^{p,\alpha}(X,d,m)\text{ contains non-constant functions}\right\}.

Let

\beta_{p}(X,d,m)=p\alpha_{p}(X,d,m).

We have a relatively cheap bound for
\beta_{p}(X,d,m)
as follows.

Proposition 2.4
.

Let
(X,d,m)
be a metric measure space satisfying
CC
and
V(
d_{h}
)
. Then

(1)

(
[
14
, Theorem 4.1]
)

p\leq\beta_{p}(X,d,m).

(2)

(
[
51
, Theorem 4.8]
)

\beta_{p}(X,d,m)\leq d_{h}+(p-1).

Remark 2.5
.

The bound
\beta_{p}(X,d,m)\leq d_{h}+p
was proved in
[
14
, Theorem 4.2]
, and the bound
\beta_{p}(X,d,m)\leq d_{h}+(p-1)
was left as a question in
[
14
, Page 524, Line 3]
. The proof of (2) in
[
51
]
proceeds similarly to the proof of
[
28
, Theorem 4.8 (ii)]
, where the case
p=2
was considered.

A direct but important consequence of Theorem
2.3
is that the converse of Proposition
2.4
holds as follows.

Corollary 2.6
.

For any
d_{h},\beta_{p}>0
satisfying

p\leq\beta_{p}\leq d_{h}+(p-1),

there exists an unbounded geodesic metric measure space
(X,d,m)
satisfying
V(
d_{h}
)
such that
\beta_{p}(X,d,m)=\beta_{p}
.

Proof.

Let
(X,d,m)
,
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
,
\Gamma
be given by Theorem
2.3
, on which
V(
d_{h}
)
,
PI(
\beta_{p}
)
and
\text{cap}(\beta_{p})_{\leq}
hold. By
[
51
, Theorem 4.6]
, the conjunction of
VD
,
PI(
\beta_{p}
)
,
\text{cap}(\beta_{p})_{\leq}
implies
\beta_{p}(X,d,m)=\beta_{p}
.
∎

This paper is organized as follows. In Section
3
, we give the proof of Proposition
2.1
. In Section
4
, we construct an
\mathbb{R}
-tree, introduce a
p
-energy with a
p
-energy measure, and prove the Poincaré inequality, the capacity upper bound and the cutoff Sobolev inequality. In Section
5
, we introduce a Laakso-type space along with a geodesic metric. In Section
6
, we introduce a
p
-energy with a
p
-energy measure on Laakso-type spaces, and prove the Poincaré inequality, the capacity upper bound and the cutoff Sobolev inequality. In Section
7
, we give the proof of Theorem
2.3
. In Section
8
, we provide some necessary results from potential theory.

3
Proof of Proposition
2.1

We start with the proof of the upper bound. We need the following result to pick up approximately evenly spaced points.

Lemma 3.1

(
[
28
, Lemma 4.10]
)
.

Let
\{x_{k}:0\leq k\leq n\}
be a sequence of points in
X
. Let
\rho>0
. Assume that
d(x_{0},x_{n})>2\rho
and

d(x_{k},x_{k-1})<\rho\text{ for any }k=1,\ldots,n,

Then there exists a subsequence
\{x_{k_{i}}:0\leq i\leq l\}
such that

(a)

0=k_{0}<k_{1}<\ldots<k_{l}=n
.

(b)

d(x_{k_{i-1}},x_{k_{i}})<5\rho
for any
i=1,\ldots,l
.

(c)

d(x_{k_{i}},x_{k_{j}})\geq 2\rho
for any distinct
i,j=0,1,\ldots,l
.

Proof of the upper bound.

For notational convenience, we may assume that
\mathrm{diam}(X)=+\infty
. For any
R,r>0
with
r\leq R
. If
R/r\lesssim 1
, then since
\Psi
is doubling and
\Phi
is increasing, we have

\frac{\Psi(R)}{\Psi(r)}\lesssim 1\leq\left(\frac{R}{r}\right)^{p-1}\frac{\Phi(R)}{\Phi(r)}.

Assume that
R/r\gtrsim 1
. Let
N
be the integer satisfying
Nr\leq R<(N+1)r
, then
N\gtrsim 1
. Take
x,y\in X
satisfying
Nr<d(x,y)<(N+1)r
. By

⁢
cap

(
Ψ
)

≤

, there exists
u\in\mathcal{F}
with
u=1
in
B(x,R/(2A_{cap}))
and
u=0
on
X\backslash B(x,R/2)
such that

\mathcal{E}(u)\leq 2C_{cap}\frac{V(x,\frac{R}{2A_{cap}})}{\Psi(\frac{R}{2A_{cap}})}\asymp\frac{\Phi(R)}{\Psi(R)}.

By
CC
, there exists a sequence
\{y_{k}:0\leq k\leq 10N\}
with
y_{0}=x
and
y_{10N}=y
such that

d(y_{k},y_{k-1})\leq C_{cc}\frac{d(x,y)}{10N}\leq C_{cc}\frac{(N+1)r}{10N}<C_{cc}\frac{2Nr}{10N}=\frac{1}{5}C_{cc}r\text{ for any }k=1,\ldots,10N.

Since
d(y_{0},y_{10N})=d(x,y)>Nr
, by Lemma
3.1
, there exists a subsequence of
\{y_{k}:0\leq k\leq 10N\}
, denoted as
\{x_{k}:0\leq k\leq M\}
such that
x_{0}=x
,
x_{M}=y
,
d(x_{k},x_{k-1})<C_{cc}r
for any
k=1,\ldots,M
and
d(x_{k},x_{l})\geq\frac{2}{5}C_{cc}r
for any distinct
k,l=0,1,\ldots,M
. Obviously,
M\leq 10N
. On the other hand

Nr<d(x,y)=d(x_{0},x_{M})\leq\sum_{k=1}^{M}d(x_{k},x_{k-1})\leq MC_{cc}r,

hence
M\geq N/C_{cc}
, which gives
M\asymp N\asymp R/r
.

For any
k=0,\ldots,M
, let
B_{k}=B(x_{k},r)
, then
u\equiv 1
in
B_{0}
and
u\equiv 0
in
B_{M}
, which gives
u_{B_{0}}=1
and
u_{B_{M}}=0
. For any
k=1,\ldots,M
, by
V(

Φ

)
,
PI(

Ψ

)
, and Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle|u_{B_{k}}-u_{B_{k-1}}|\leq\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B_{k}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B_{k-1}}|u(x)-u(y)|^{p}m(\mathrm{d}y)m(\mathrm{d}x)\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(m(B_{k})m(B_{k-1}))^{1/p}}\left(\int_{B(x_{k},2C_{cc}r)}\int_{{B(x_{k},2C_{cc}r)}}|u(x)-u(y)|^{p}m(\mathrm{d}y)m(\mathrm{d}x)\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\Phi(r)^{1/p}}\left(\int_{B(x_{k},2C_{cc}r)}|u-u_{B(x_{k},2C_{cc}r)}|^{p}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\Psi(r)}{\Phi(r)}\right)^{1/p}\Gamma(u)(B(x_{k},2A_{PI}C_{cc}r))^{1/p},

hence

\displaystyle 1=|u_{B_{0}}-u_{B_{M}}|\leq\sum_{k=1}^{M}|u_{B_{k}}-u_{B_{k-1}}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\Psi(r)}{\Phi(r)}\right)^{1/p}\sum_{k=1}^{M}\Gamma(u)(B(x_{k},2A_{PI}C_{cc}r))^{1/p}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\Psi(r)}{\Phi(r)}\right)^{1/p}M^{1-{1}/{p}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{M}\Gamma(u)(B(x_{k},2A_{PI}C_{cc}r))\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\asymp\left(\frac{\Psi(r)}{\Phi(r)}\right)^{1/p}\left(\frac{R}{r}\right)^{1-{1}/{p}}\left(\int_{X}\sum_{k=1}^{M}1_{B(x_{k},2A_{PI}C_{cc}r)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma(u)\right)^{1/p}.

Since
d(x_{k},x_{l})\geq\frac{2}{5}C_{cc}r
for any distinct
k,l=0,1,\ldots,M
, by
V(

Φ

)
, there exists some positive integer
K
depending only on
C_{\Phi},A_{PI},C_{cc}
such that

\sum_{k=1}^{M}1_{B(x_{k},2A_{PI}C_{cc}r)}\leq K1_{\cup_{k=1}^{M}B(x_{k},2A_{PI}C_{cc}r)}.

Hence

1\lesssim\left(\frac{\Psi(r)}{\Phi(r)}\right)^{1/p}\left(\frac{R}{r}\right)^{1-{1}/{p}}\mathcal{E}(u)^{1/p}\lesssim\left(\frac{\Psi(r)}{\Phi(r)}\right)^{1/p}\left(\frac{R}{r}\right)^{1-{1}/{p}}\left(\frac{\Phi(R)}{\Psi(R)}\right)^{1/p},

which gives

\frac{\Psi(R)}{\Psi(r)}\lesssim\left(\frac{R}{r}\right)^{p-1}\frac{\Phi(R)}{\Phi(r)}.

∎

We provide two proofs of the lower bound via the implications (
2.2
) and (
2.3
). To this end, we make several preparations. Let
\varepsilon>0
. We say that
V\subseteq X
is an
\varepsilon
-net if for any distinct
x,y\in V
, we have
d(x,y)\geq\varepsilon
, and for any
z\in X
, there exists
x\in V
such that
d(x,z)<\varepsilon
. Since
(X,d)
is separable, all
\varepsilon
-nets are countable.

We have the following partition of unity with controlled energy.

Lemma 3.2
.

Assume that
VD
and

⁢
cap

(
Ψ
)

≤

hold. Then we have the following controlled cutoff condition. There exists
C_{cut}>0
depending only on
p,C_{\Psi},C_{VD},C_{cap},A_{cap}
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\mathrm{diam}(X))
, for any
\varepsilon
-net
V
, there exists a family of functions
\{\psi_{z}\in\mathcal{F}:z\in V\}
satisfying the following conditions.

(CO1)

For any
z\in V
,
0\leq\psi_{z}\leq 1
in
X
,
\psi_{z}=1
in
B(z,\varepsilon/4)
, and
\psi_{z}=0
on
X\backslash B(z,5\varepsilon/4)
.

(CO2)

\sum_{z\in V}\psi_{z}=1
.

(CO3)

For any
z\in V
,
\mathcal{E}(\psi_{z})\leq C_{cut}\frac{V(z,\varepsilon)}{\Psi(\varepsilon)}
.

Remark 3.3
.

By
VD
, in any bounded open set, the summation in
(CO2)
is indeed a finite summation.

The proof is essentially the same as the proof of
[
41
, Lemma 2.5]
for
p=2
. We give the proof here for completeness.

Proof.

For any
z\in V
, let

R_{z}=\{x\in X:d(x,z)=d(x,V)\},

then
B(z,\varepsilon/2)\subseteq R_{z}\subseteq B(z,\varepsilon)
and
\{R_{z}:z\in V\}
is a cover of
X
. Let
N_{z}
be an
(\varepsilon/(4A_{cap}))
-net of
R_{z}
. By
VD
, there exists some positive integer
M_{1}
depending only on
C_{VD},A_{cap}
such that
\#N_{z}\leq M_{1}
. For any
w\in N_{z}
, by
VD
and

⁢
cap

(
Ψ
)

≤

, there exists
\rho_{w}\in\mathcal{F}
with
0\leq\rho_{w}\leq 1
in
X
,
\rho_{w}=1
in
B(w,\varepsilon/(4A_{cap}))
,
\rho_{w}=0
on
X\backslash B(w,\varepsilon/4)
such that

\mathcal{E}(\rho_{w})\leq 2C_{cap}\frac{V(w,\varepsilon/(4A_{cap}))}{\Psi(\varepsilon/(4A_{cap}))}\leq C_{1}\frac{V(z,\varepsilon)}{\Psi(\varepsilon)},

where
C_{1}>0
depends only on
C_{\Psi},C_{VD},C_{cap},A_{cap}
.

For any
z\in V
, let
\varphi_{z}=\max_{w\in N_{z}}\rho_{w}
, then
0\leq\varphi_{z}\leq 1
in
X
,
\varphi_{z}=1
on
R_{z}
,
\varphi_{z}=0
on
\{x\in X:d(x,R_{z})\geq\varepsilon/4\}
. By
SubAdd
, we have
\varphi_{z}\in\mathcal{F}
and

\mathcal{E}(\varphi_{z})\leq C\sum_{w\in N_{z}}\mathcal{E}(\rho_{w})\leq CM_{1}C_{1}\frac{V(z,\varepsilon)}{\Psi(\varepsilon)}=C_{2}\frac{V(z,\varepsilon)}{\Psi(\varepsilon)},

where
C>0
depends only on
p,M_{1}
, and
C_{2}=CM_{1}C_{1}
. Since
\{R_{z}:z\in V\}
is a cover of
X
, we have
\sum_{z\in V}\varphi_{z}\geq 1
in
X
. By
VD
, there exists some positive integer
M_{2}
depending only on
C_{VD}
such that
\#\{w\in V:\varphi_{w}\neq 0\text{ in }B(z,\frac{5}{4}\varepsilon)\}\leq M_{2}
for any
z\in V
, which implies that
\sum_{z\in V}\varphi_{z}\leq M_{2}
in
X
.

For any
z\in V
, let
\psi_{z}=\frac{\varphi_{z}}{\sum_{z\in V}\varphi_{z}}
. Since
\varphi_{z}=0
on
X\backslash B(z,5\varepsilon/4)
, we have

\psi_{z}=\frac{\varphi_{z}}{\sum_{w\in V:\varphi_{w}\neq 0\text{ in }B(z,\frac{5}{4}\varepsilon)}\varphi_{w}}.

By
[
51
, Proposition 2.3 (c)]
, we have
\psi_{z}\in\mathcal{F}
, and there exists
C_{3}>0
depending only on
p,M_{2}
such that

\mathcal{E}(\psi_{z})\leq C_{3}\sum_{w\in V:\varphi_{w}\neq 0\text{ in }B(z,\frac{5}{4}\varepsilon)}\mathcal{E}(\varphi_{w})\leq C_{3}M_{2}C_{2}\frac{V(z,\varepsilon)}{\Psi(\varepsilon)}=C_{4}\frac{V(z,\varepsilon)}{\Psi(\varepsilon)},

where
C_{4}=C_{3}M_{2}C_{2}
, hence we have
(CO3)
.

For any distinct
z,w\in V
, we claim that

B(w,\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon)\subseteq\{x\in X:d(x,R_{z})\geq\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon\}.

Otherwise there exists
x\in X
such that
d(x,w)<\varepsilon/4
,
d(x,R_{z})<\varepsilon/4
, there exists
y\in R_{z}
such that
d(x,y)<\varepsilon/4
, then
d(y,w)\leq d(x,y)+d(x,w)<\varepsilon/2
. But
y\in R_{z}
implies
d(y,z)=d(y,V)\leq d(y,w)<\varepsilon/2
, hence
d(z,w)\leq d(y,z)+d(y,w)<\varepsilon
, contradicting to that
V
is an
\varepsilon
-net. Hence for any
z\in V
,
\sum_{z\in V}\varphi_{z}=\varphi_{z}
in
B(z,\varepsilon/4)
,
\psi_{z}=\varphi_{z}/\varphi_{z}=1
in
B(z,\varepsilon/4)
,
\psi_{z}=0
on
\{x\in X:d(x,R_{z})\geq\varepsilon/4\}\supseteq X\backslash B(z,5\varepsilon/4)
, hence we have
(CO1)
.

Finally,
(CO2)
is obvious.
∎

Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{loc}=\left\{u:\begin{array}[]{l}\text{for any relatively compact open set }U,\\
\text{there exists }u^{\#}\in\mathcal{F}\text{ such that }u=u^{\#}\text{ }m\text{-a.e. in }U\end{array}\right\}.

For any
u\in\mathcal{F}_{loc}
, let
\Gamma(u)|_{U}=\Gamma(u^{\#})|_{U}
, where
u^{\#}
,
U
are given as above, then
\Gamma(u)
is a well-defined positive Radon measure on
X
.

We have a characterization of
PI(

Ψ

)
in terms of maximal functions as follows.

Lemma 3.4
.

Assume that
VD
and
PI(

Ψ

)
hold. Then there exist
C_{max}>0
,
A_{max}>1
such that for any
u\in\mathcal{F}_{loc}
, for any ball
B
with radius
R
, for
m
-a.e.
x,y\in A_{max}^{-1}B
, we have

|u(x)-u(y)|^{p}\leq C_{max}\Psi(d(x,y))\left(M_{R}\Gamma(u)(x)+M_{R}\Gamma(u)(y)\right),

where

M_{R}\Gamma(u)(x)=\sup_{r\in(0,R)}\frac{\Gamma(u)(B(x,r))}{V(x,r)}.

Proof.

The proof is standard using the telescopic technique. Since
u\in\mathcal{F}_{loc}
, we have
u\in L^{p}_{loc}(X;m)
,
m
-almost all points are Lebesgue points of
u
. Let
x,y\in(4A_{PI})^{-1}B
be two distinct Lebesgue points of
u
. Denote
D=d(x,y)\in(0,R/(2A_{PI}))
. For any
n\geq 0
, let
B_{n}=B(x,2^{-n}D)
, then by
VD
,
PI(

Ψ

)
, and Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle|u_{B_{n}}-u_{B_{n+1}}|\lesssim\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B_{n}}|u-u_{B_{n}}|^{p}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{1/p}\lesssim\left(\frac{\Psi(2^{-n}D)}{m(B_{n})}\int_{A_{PI}B_{n}}\mathrm{d}\Gamma(u)\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\Psi(2^{-n}D)\frac{\Gamma(u)(A_{PI}B_{n})}{m(A_{PI}B_{n})}\right)^{1/p}\leq\left(\Psi(2^{-n}D)M_{R}\Gamma(u)(x)\right)^{1/p},

hence

\displaystyle|u(x)-u_{B(x,D)}|=\lim_{n\to+\infty}|u_{B_{n}}-u_{B_{0}}|\leq\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}|u_{B_{n}}-u_{B_{n+1}}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(\Psi(2^{-n}D)M_{R}\Gamma(u)(x)\right)^{1/p}\lesssim\left(\Psi(D)M_{R}\Gamma(u)(x)\right)^{1/p},

where the last inequality follows from Equation (
2.1
). Similarly, we have

|u(y)-u_{B(y,D)}|\lesssim\left(\Psi(D)M_{R}\Gamma(u)(y)\right)^{1/p}.

Moreover

\displaystyle|u_{B(x,D)}-u_{B(y,D)}|\leq\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x,D)}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(y,D)}|u(z_{1})-u(z_{2})|^{p}m(\mathrm{d}z_{1})m(\mathrm{d}z_{2})\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x,2D)}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B(x,2D)}|u(z_{1})-u(z_{2})|^{p}m(\mathrm{d}z_{1})m(\mathrm{d}z_{2})\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{1}{V(x,2D)}\int_{B(x,2D)}|u-u_{B(x,2D)}|^{p}\mathrm{d}m\right)^{1/p}\lesssim\left(\frac{\Psi(2D)}{V(x,2D)}\int_{B(x,2A_{PI}D)}\mathrm{d}\Gamma(u)\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\Psi(D)\frac{\Gamma(u)(B(x,2A_{PI}D))}{V(x,2A_{PI}D)}\right)^{1/p}\leq\left(\Psi(D)M_{R}\Gamma(u)(x)\right)^{1/p}.

In summary, we have

\displaystyle|u(x)-u(y)|\leq|u(x)-u_{B(x,D)}|+|u(y)-u_{B(y,D)}|+|u_{B(x,D)}-u_{B(y,D)}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Psi(D)^{1/p}\left(M_{R}\Gamma(u)(x)^{1/p}+M_{R}\Gamma(u)(y)^{1/p}\right),

which implies

|u(x)-u(y)|^{p}\lesssim\Psi(D)\left(M_{R}\Gamma(u)(x)+M_{R}\Gamma(u)(y)\right).

∎

Let
\varepsilon>0
and
x,y\in X
. We say that
\{x_{k}:0\leq k\leq N\}
is an
\varepsilon
-chain between
x
and
y
if
x_{0}=x
,
x_{N}=y
, and
d(x_{k},x_{k-1})<\varepsilon
for any
k=1,\ldots,N
. Let

N_{\varepsilon}(x,y)=\inf\left\{N:\{x_{k}:0\leq k\leq N\}\text{ is an }\varepsilon\text{-chain between }x\text{ and }y\right\},

where
\inf\emptyset=+\infty
, see
[
31
, Section 6]
for more details. By the triangle inequality, we have
N_{\varepsilon}(x,y)\geq\frac{d(x,y)}{\varepsilon}
. Assume
CC
, then
N_{\varepsilon}(x,y)\leq C_{cc}\frac{d(x,y)}{\varepsilon}+1
, hence
N_{\varepsilon}(x,y)\asymp\frac{d(x,y)}{\varepsilon}
.

Proof of the lower bound through the implication (
2.2
).

For notational convenience, we may assume that
\mathrm{diam}(X)=+\infty
. For any
R,\varepsilon>0
with
\varepsilon\leq R
. If
R/\varepsilon\lesssim 1
, then since
\Psi
is increasing, we have

\left(\frac{R}{\varepsilon}\right)^{p}\lesssim 1\leq\frac{\Psi(R)}{\Psi(\varepsilon)}.

We show that if
R/\varepsilon\gtrsim 1
, then

\frac{\Psi(R)}{\Psi(\varepsilon)}\gtrsim\left(\frac{R}{\varepsilon}\right)^{p}.

Take
\overline{x},\overline{y}\in X
with
R<d(\overline{x},\overline{y})<R+\varepsilon
, take an
\varepsilon
-net
V
with
\{\overline{x},\overline{y}\}\in V
, let
\{\psi_{z}:z\in V\}
be the family of functions given by Lemma
3.2
, let

u=\sum_{z\in V}N_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x},z)\psi_{z}.

By
(CO1)
, we have
u\equiv u(\overline{x})=N_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x},\overline{x})=0
in
B(\overline{x},\varepsilon/4)
and
u\equiv u(\overline{y})=N_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x},\overline{y})\asymp\frac{d(\overline{x},\overline{y})}{\varepsilon}\asymp\frac{R}{\varepsilon}
in
B(\overline{y},\varepsilon/4)
. By
VD
, the above summation is locally a finite summation of functions in
\mathcal{F}
, hence
u\in\mathcal{F}_{loc}
.

Firstly, we show that for any
\overline{z}\in V

\Gamma(u)(B(\overline{z},\frac{5}{4}\varepsilon))\lesssim\frac{V(\overline{z},\varepsilon)}{\Psi(\varepsilon)}.

Let
N_{\overline{z}}=\{z\in V:B(z,\frac{5}{4}\varepsilon)\cap B(\overline{z},\frac{5}{4}\varepsilon)\neq\emptyset\}
. By
VD
, there exists some positive integer
N
depending only on
C_{VD}
such that
\#N_{\overline{z}}\leq N
. By
(CO2)
, we have

u=\sum_{z\in V}N_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x},z)\psi_{z}=\sum_{z\in V}\left(N_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x},z)-N_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x},\overline{z})\right)\psi_{z}+N_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x},\overline{z}).

In particular, in
B(\overline{z},\frac{5}{4}\varepsilon)
, by
(CO1)
, we have

u=\sum_{z\in N_{\overline{z}}}\left(N_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x},z)-N_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x},\overline{z})\right)\psi_{z}+N_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x},\overline{z}),

hence

\displaystyle\Gamma(u)(B(\overline{z},\frac{5}{4}\varepsilon))=\Gamma\left(\sum_{z\in N_{\overline{z}}}\left(N_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x},z)-N_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x},\overline{z})\right)\psi_{z}+N_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x},\overline{z})\right)(B(\overline{z},\frac{5}{4}\varepsilon))

\displaystyle=\Gamma\left(\sum_{z\in N_{\overline{z}}}\left(N_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x},z)-N_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x},\overline{z})\right)\psi_{z}\right)(B(\overline{z},\frac{5}{4}\varepsilon))

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\sum_{z\in N_{\overline{z}}}\Gamma\left(\left(N_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x},z)-N_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x},\overline{z})\right)\psi_{z}\right)(B(\overline{z},\frac{5}{4}\varepsilon))

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\sum_{z\in N_{\overline{z}}}|N_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x},z)-N_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x},\overline{z})|^{p}\mathcal{E}(\psi_{z}),

where
C_{1}>0
depends only on
p,N
. For any
z\in N_{\overline{z}}
, we have
d(z,\overline{z})<\frac{5}{2}\varepsilon
, then by
CC
, we have
|N_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x},z)-N_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x},\overline{z})|\leq\lceil{\frac{5}{2}C_{cc}}\rceil
. Combining this with
(CO3)
and
VD
, we have

\displaystyle\Gamma(u)(B(\overline{z},\frac{5}{4}\varepsilon))\leq C_{1}\sum_{z\in N_{\overline{z}}}\lceil{\frac{5}{2}C_{cc}}\rceil^{p}C_{cut}\frac{V(z,\varepsilon)}{\Psi(\varepsilon)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\sum_{z\in N_{\overline{z}}}\lceil{\frac{5}{2}C_{cc}}\rceil^{p}C_{cut}C_{2}\frac{V(\overline{z},\varepsilon)}{\Psi(\varepsilon)}\leq C_{3}\frac{V(\overline{z},\varepsilon)}{\Psi(\varepsilon)},

where
C_{2}>0
depends only on
C_{VD}
, and
C_{3}=\lceil{\frac{5}{2}C_{cc}}\rceil^{p}NC_{1}C_{2}C_{cut}
.

Secondly, we show that for any
\overline{z}\in V
and any
r>0

\Gamma(u)(B(\overline{z},r))\lesssim\frac{V(\overline{z},r)}{\Psi(\varepsilon)}.

If
r\leq\varepsilon/4
, then in
B(\overline{z},r)
, we have
u\equiv N_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x},\overline{z})
, hence
\Gamma(u)(B(\overline{z},r))=0
, the result is trivial. We may assume that
r>\varepsilon/4
, then

\displaystyle\Gamma(u)(B(\overline{z},r))\leq\sum_{z\in V:B(z,\frac{5}{4}\varepsilon)\cap B(\overline{z},r)\neq\emptyset}\Gamma(u)(B(z,\frac{5}{4}\varepsilon))

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{z\in V:B(z,\frac{5}{4}\varepsilon)\cap B(\overline{z},r)\neq\emptyset}C_{3}\frac{V(z,\varepsilon)}{\Psi(\varepsilon)}=\frac{C_{3}}{\Psi(\varepsilon)}\int_{X}\sum_{z\in V:B(z,\frac{5}{4}\varepsilon)\cap B(\overline{z},r)\neq\emptyset}1_{B(z,\varepsilon)}\mathrm{d}m.

By
VD
, there exists some positive integer
M
depending only on
C_{VD}
such that

\sum_{z\in V:B(z,\frac{5}{4}\varepsilon)\cap B(\overline{z},r)\neq\emptyset}1_{B(z,\varepsilon)}\leq M1_{\cup_{z\in V:B(z,\frac{5}{4}\varepsilon)\cap B(\overline{z},r)\neq\emptyset}B(z,\varepsilon)},

hence

\displaystyle\Gamma(u)(B(\overline{z},r))\leq\frac{C_{3}}{\Psi(\varepsilon)}Mm\left({\bigcup_{z\in V:B(z,\frac{5}{4}\varepsilon)\cap B(\overline{z},r)\neq\emptyset}B(z,\varepsilon)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{3}M}{\Psi(\varepsilon)}m(B(\overline{z},r+\frac{5}{2}\varepsilon))\leq\frac{C_{3}M}{\Psi(\varepsilon)}m(B(\overline{z},11r))\leq\frac{C_{4}}{\Psi(\varepsilon)}V(\overline{z},r),

where
C_{4}=C_{3}C_{VD}^{4}M
.

Finally, in Lemma
3.4
, let
B=B(\overline{x},4A_{max}R)
, then
\overline{x},\overline{y}\in A_{max}^{-1}B=B(\overline{x},4R)
, since
B(\overline{x},\frac{\varepsilon}{4})\cup B(\overline{y},\frac{\varepsilon}{4})\subseteq B(\overline{x},4R)
, and
u\equiv u(\overline{x})=N_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x},\overline{x})=0
in
B(\overline{x},\frac{\varepsilon}{4})
,
u\equiv u(\overline{y})=N_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x},\overline{y})\asymp\frac{R}{\varepsilon}
in
B(\overline{y},\frac{\varepsilon}{4})
, we may assume that
\overline{x},\overline{y}
are Lebesgue points of
u
, then

\displaystyle|u(\overline{x})-u(\overline{y})|^{p}\leq C_{max}\Psi(d(\overline{x},\overline{y}))\left(M_{4A_{max}R}\Gamma(u)(\overline{x})+M_{4A_{max}R}\Gamma(u)(\overline{y})\right),

where

\displaystyle M_{4A_{max}R}\Gamma(u)(\overline{x})=\sup_{r\in(0,4A_{max}R)}\frac{\Gamma(u)(B(\overline{x},r))}{V(\overline{x},r)}\leq\frac{C_{4}}{\Psi(\varepsilon)},

\displaystyle M_{4A_{max}R}\Gamma(u)(\overline{y})=\sup_{r\in(0,4A_{max}R)}\frac{\Gamma(u)(B(\overline{y},r))}{V(\overline{y},r)}\leq\frac{C_{4}}{\Psi(\varepsilon)}.

Hence

\left(\frac{R}{\varepsilon}\right)^{p}\asymp N_{\varepsilon}(\overline{x},\overline{y})^{p}=|u(\overline{x})-u(\overline{y})|^{p}\leq C_{max}\Psi(d(\overline{x},\overline{y}))\frac{2C_{4}}{\Psi(\varepsilon)}\asymp\frac{\Psi(R)}{\Psi(\varepsilon)},

which gives

\left(\frac{R}{\varepsilon}\right)^{p}\lesssim\frac{\Psi(R)}{\Psi(\varepsilon)}.

∎

We need the following capacity upper bound.

Lemma 3.5
.

Assume that
VD
and

⁢
cap

(
Ψ
)

≤

hold. Then there exists
C>0
such that for any
x\in X
and any
R,r>0
, we have

\mathrm{cap}(B(x,R),X\backslash B(x,R+r))\leq C\frac{m\left(B(x,R+r)\backslash\overline{B(x,R)}\right)}{\Psi(r)}.

Proof.

Let
C_{1}=C_{cap}
,
A=A_{cap}
be the constants in

⁢
cap

(
Ψ
)

≤

. Let
L=4A+4
and let
V
be an
\frac{r}{L}
-net. For any
v\in V
, by

⁢
cap

(
Ψ
)

≤

, there exists a cutoff function
\phi_{v}\in\mathcal{F}
for
B(v,\frac{r}{L})\subseteq B(v,\frac{Ar}{L})
such that

\mathcal{E}(\phi_{v})=\int_{B(v,\frac{Ar}{L})}\mathrm{d}\Gamma(\phi_{v})\leq 2C_{1}\frac{V(v,\frac{r}{L})}{\Psi(\frac{r}{L})}.

Let
{V}_{1}=V\cap B(x,R+\frac{r}{2})
, then by
VD
, we have
\#V_{1}<+\infty
. Let

\phi=\max_{v\in{V}_{1}}\phi_{v},

then by
SubAdd
, we have
\phi\in\mathcal{F}
. Moreover,
0\leq\phi\leq 1
in
X
,
\phi=1
in
B(x,R+\frac{r}{2}-\frac{r}{L})\supseteq\overline{B(x,R)}
,
\mathrm{supp}(\phi)\subseteq B(x,R+\frac{r}{2}+\frac{Ar}{L})\subseteq B(x,R+r)
, hence
\phi\in\mathcal{F}
is a cutoff function for
B(x,R)\subseteq B(x,R+r)
and

\mathrm{supp}(\Gamma(\phi))\subseteq B(x,R+\frac{r}{2}+\frac{Ar}{L})\backslash{B(x,R+\frac{r}{2}-\frac{r}{L})}.

Let
W={V}_{1}\backslash B(x,R+\frac{r}{2}-\frac{r}{L}-\frac{Ar}{L})
, then

\phi=\max_{v\in W}\phi_{v}\text{ in }B(x,R+\frac{r}{2}+\frac{Ar}{L})\backslash{B(x,R+\frac{r}{2}-\frac{r}{L})},

hence

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\phi)=\int_{B(x,R+\frac{r}{2}+\frac{Ar}{L})\backslash{B(x,R+\frac{r}{2}-\frac{r}{L})}}\mathrm{d}\Gamma(\phi)\overset{(\star)}{\scalebox{2}[1]{$\leq$}}\sum_{v\in W}\int_{{B(x,R+\frac{r}{2}+\frac{Ar}{L})\backslash{B(x,R+\frac{r}{2}-\frac{r}{L})}}}\mathrm{d}\Gamma(\phi_{v})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{v\in W}\int_{{B(v,\frac{Ar}{L})}}\mathrm{d}\Gamma(\phi_{v})\leq 2C_{1}\sum_{v\in W}\frac{V(v,\frac{r}{L})}{\Psi(\frac{r}{L})}=\frac{2C_{1}}{\Psi(\frac{r}{L})}\int_{X}\left(\sum_{v\in W}1_{B(v,\frac{r}{L})}\right)\mathrm{d}m,

where
(\star)
follows from
SubAdd
. By
VD
, there exists some positive integer
N
depending only on
C_{VD}
such that

\sum_{v\in W}1_{B(v,\frac{r}{L})}\leq N1_{\cup_{v\in W}B(v,\frac{r}{L})},

where

{\bigcup_{v\in W}B(v,\frac{r}{L})}\subseteq B(x,R+\frac{r}{2}+\frac{r}{L})\backslash\overline{B(x,R+\frac{r}{2}-\frac{r}{L}-\frac{Ar}{L}-\frac{r}{L})}\subseteq B(x,R+r)\backslash\overline{B(x,R)}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{cap}\left(B(x,R),X\backslash B(x,R+r)\right)\leq\mathcal{E}(\phi)\leq\frac{2C_{1}N}{\Psi(\frac{r}{L})}m\left(B(x,R+r)\backslash\overline{B(x,R)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2C_{1}NC_{2}}{\Psi({r})}m\left(B(x,R+r)\backslash\overline{B(x,R)}\right)=C\frac{m\left(B(x,R+r)\backslash\overline{B(x,R)}\right)}{\Psi(r)},

where
C_{2}>0
depends only on
C_{\Psi},L
, and
C=2C_{1}NC_{2}
.
∎

Proof of the lower bound through the implication (
2.3
).

For notational convenience, we may assume that
\mathrm{diam}(X)=+\infty
. Let
A=A_{cap}
be the constant in
\text{cap}(\Psi)
. For any
R,r>0
with
r\leq R
, without loss of generality, we may assume that
R/r\gtrsim 1
, let
N
be the integer satisfying
Nr\leq(A-1)R<(N+1)r
, then
N\asymp R/r\gtrsim 1
. By
VD
and

⁢
cap

(
Ψ
)

≤

, applying Lemma
3.5
, there exists
C_{1}>0
such that for any
n=0,\ldots,N-1
, there exists a cutoff function
\phi_{n}\in\mathcal{F}
for
B(x,R+nr)\subseteq B(x,R+(n+1)r)
such that

\mathcal{E}(\phi_{n})\leq C_{1}\frac{m\left(B(x,R+(n+1)r)\backslash\overline{B(x,R+nr)}\right)}{\Psi(r)}.

Let
\phi=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\phi_{n}
, then
\phi\in\mathcal{F}
is a cutoff function for
B(x,R)\subseteq B(x,R+Nr)
, hence also a cutoff function for
B(x,R)\subseteq B(x,AR)
. By

⁢
cap

(
Ψ
)

≥

, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{C_{cap}}\frac{V(x,R)}{\Psi(R)}\leq\mathrm{cap}\left(B(x,R),X\backslash B(x,AR)\right)\leq\mathcal{E}(\phi)\overset{(\star)}{\scalebox{2}[1]{$\leq$}}\frac{1}{N^{p}}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\mathcal{E}\left(\phi_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}}{N^{p}}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\frac{m\left(B(x,R+(n+1)r)\backslash\overline{B(x,R+nr)}\right)}{\Psi(r)}\leq\frac{C_{1}}{N^{p}}\frac{V(x,AR)}{\Psi(r)}\overset{(\diamond)}{\scalebox{2}[1]{$\leq$}}\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{N^{p}}\frac{V(x,R)}{\Psi(r)},

where
(\star)
follows from
SubAdd
,
(\diamond)
follows from
VD
, and
C_{2}>0
depends only on
C_{VD},A
. Therefore, we have

\frac{\Psi(R)}{\Psi(r)}\gtrsim N^{p}\asymp\left(\frac{R}{r}\right)^{p}.

∎

4
Construction of
\mathbb{R}
-trees

In this section, we construct an
\mathbb{R}
-tree, introduce a
p
-energy with a
p
-energy measure, and prove the Poincaré inequality, the capacity upper bound and the cutoff Sobolev inequality.

Fix a branching function
\mathbf{b}:\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{N}
, which satisfies

2\leq\inf_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{b}(k)\leq\sup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{b}(k)<+\infty,

and a gluing function
\mathbf{g}:\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{N}
, which satisfies

1\leq\inf_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{g}(k)\leq\sup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{g}(k)<+\infty.

Let

\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{g})=\left\{\mathbf{s}:\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{Z}|\mathbf{s}(k)\in\{0,1,\ldots,\mathbf{g}(k)-1\}\text{ for any }k\in\mathbb{Z}\text{ and }\lim_{k\to+\infty}\mathbf{s}(k)=0\right\}.

For any distinct
\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{g})
, let
\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{g})}(\mathbf{t},\mathbf{t})=0
and

\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{g})}(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t})=2^{\max\{k\in\mathbb{Z}:\mathbf{s}(k)\neq\mathbf{t}(k)\}}.

Then
(\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{g}),\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{g})})
is an ultrametric space, that is, it is a metric space and the metric satisfies the following inequality.

\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{g})}(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t})\leq\max\left\{\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{g})}(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{r}),\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{g})}(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{t})\right\}\text{ for any }\mathbf{r},\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{g}).

Let us list some “unusual” properties of ultrametric spaces as follows, which are easy consequences of the above inequality.

•

Any two balls with the same radius are either disjoint or coincide. More generally, any two balls are either disjoint or one contains the other.

•

Any point inside a ball is its center. Any ball is simultaneously open and closed.

For any
\mathbf{s}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{g})
and any
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, the ball
\{\mathbf{t}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{g}):\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{g})}(\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s})\leq 2^{n}\}
coincides with the cylindrical set
\{\mathbf{t}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{g}):\mathbf{t}(k)=\mathbf{s}(k)\text{ for any }k\geq n+1\}
. For any
r>0
, let

V_{\mathbf{g}}(r)=\begin{cases}\left(\prod_{k=n}^{0}\mathbf{g}(k)\right)^{-1}&\text{if }2^{n-1}\leq r<2^{n},n\leq 0,\\
1&\text{if }1\leq r<2,\\
\prod_{k=1}^{n-1}\mathbf{g}(k)&\text{if }2^{n-1}\leq r<2^{n},n\geq 2.\end{cases}

Then there exists a unique measure
\mathbf{m}_{\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{g})}
on
(\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{g}),\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{g})})
such that any closed ball with radius
2^{n}
has measure
V_{\mathbf{g}}(2^{n})
. It is obvious that there exists some positive constant
C
depending only on
\sup_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{g}
such that

\frac{1}{C}V_{\mathbf{g}}(r)\leq\mathbf{m}_{\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{g})}(B_{\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{g})}(\mathbf{t},r))\leq CV_{\mathbf{g}}(r)\text{ for any }\mathbf{t}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{g}),r>0.

Similarly, we also have an ultrametric space
(\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{b}),\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{b})})
endowed with a measure
\mathbf{m}_{\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{b})}
, under which any closed ball with radius
2^{n}
has measure
V_{\mathbf{b}}(2^{n})
, and there exists some positive constant
C
depending only on
\sup_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{b}
such that

\frac{1}{C}V_{\mathbf{b}}(r)\leq\mathbf{m}_{\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{b})}(B_{\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{b})}(\mathbf{t},r))\leq CV_{\mathbf{b}}(r)\text{ for any }\mathbf{t}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{b}),r>0.

We construct an
\mathbb{R}
-tree
(\mathcal{T}(\mathbf{b}),\mathbf{d}_{\mathcal{T}(\mathbf{b})})
endowed with a measure
\mathbf{m}_{\mathcal{T}(\mathbf{b})}
, using approximation of finite trees. A detailed and rigorous construction was given in
[
43
, Subsections 3.1, 3.2, 3.3]
. Here we only outline the main idea as follows.

For any
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}
with
m\leq n
, we construct a finite tree
T_{m,n}
endowed with a metric
\mathbf{d}_{m,n}
inductively on
n-m
, starting from
n-m=0
. For the sets
T_{m,n}
. Firstly,
T_{n,n}
is a star graph
K_{1,\mathbf{b}(n)}
. Secondly,
T_{m,n+1}
is obtained

(TREE1)

either by gluing
\mathbf{b}(n+1)
copies of
T_{m,n}
at one vertex,

(TREE2)

or by replacing each edge of
T_{m+1,n}
by a star graph
K_{1,\mathbf{b}(m)}
.

We give a simple example of
\mathbf{b}
with
\mathbf{b}(-1)=6
,
\mathbf{b}(0)=3
,
\mathbf{b}(1)=4
, to illustrate how the construction works, see Figure
2
, Figure
3
, Figure
4
for the figures of
T_{-1,-1}
, …,
T_{-1,1}
.

(a)

T_{-1,-1}

(b)

T_{0,0}

(c)

T_{1,1}
